isect_buf;\n\tisect_buf.reserve(16);\n\n\tint stackpos = 0;\n\n\t// Reserve a conservative worst-case amount of memory to store node pairs\n\tstackobj *stack = new stackobj[std::max(edgeBvh->GetHeight(), triBvh->GetHeight()) * 2 + 1];\n\tstack[0].edgeNode = 0;\n\tstack[0].triNode = 0;\n\n\twhile ((stackpos >= 0) && (contacts.size() < maxContacts)) It's very possible there are subtle bugs and inaccuracies in\n\t\t// the BVH traversal logic that result in missed collision contacts.\n\t\t// However, this is the most performant version of this code rotAabb.Intersects(triBvh->GetNode(triNode->kids[1])->aabb);\n\n\t\t\tif (left & right) {\n\t\t\t\t// Recurse into edge nodes until we find one that's smaller than the tri node\n\t\t\t\t// (or hit a That means the scenario I fixed is my best reading of it, not something I confirmed against the recording.\n\n**What I think the action is:** a takeoff from a pad inside a rotating orbital station without the autopilot (`Player::DoFixspeedTakeoff`). The code is meant to move the ship clear of the pad and match the station's spin, and the comment says it should leave a margin. But it lifted the ship only **0.01 m** and gave it only **0.05 m/s** away from the pad. Two things go wrong:\n- The ship is placed on the pad using a landing point in its model (`tag_landing`). Its collision shape, or the pad's, can extend slightly past that point, so the two already overlap when collisions switch back on. The ship is hit at the very first frame, before you see any contact.\n- At 0.05 m/s, the station's rotation carries the pad back into the ship almost at once.\n\nAt that moment the station has already released the bay, so any contact is counted as a crash and the hull loses integrity. This happens at every pad, since every pad has the same setup.\n\n**The fix:**\n- The ship is lifted 0.5 m along the pad's up direction.\n- It then keeps moving up in 0.25 m steps until its collision shape no longer touches the station's, with a limit on the total lift. A final 0.5 m margin is added if any extra lift was needed. Because this tests the actual collision shapes, it handles any ship and station combination.\n- The outward speed is now the 2 m/s takeoff cruise speed instead of 0.05 m/s, which is fast enough to stay ahead of the rotation.\n\nThe sideways velocity that matches the station's spin is unchanged, as are docking, autopilot launches and ground stations.\n\n**One gap:** the overlap check only covers the station's main collision shape.
